- **🏷️ The station tag was still being cut in half on a phone — `Chill · ad-free` read `Chill · ad-` on one line and `free` on the next, in 82 of the catalogue's 146 rows, because the row's name column had no width and the sentence about the station took the rest**: the pass before this one fixed the *inline* case — the tag used to trail the station name, arrive as `Groove Salad Chill · ad-free`, and break inside the narrow column — by putting the line on its own row under the name. That was half the defect, and the half left behind is the one a phone sees: **the row is two flex items with no basis on either**, so it is not a share but a content-sized layout, and the longer side (the sentence) takes the whole remainder while the name column is held at its *min-content* width — the longest word in it. Measured on the deployed page at 375px it came out **55px of a 285px row**, at 480px **68px of a 390px row**, and on a desktop **117px of a 651px row**; at 55px a line breaker sees `ad-` as the last thing that fits. **The same row shape was doing the same thing to the command list** — `/music radio` split across two lines in a 77px column — so the fix is the shape rather than the tag: `ul.lp-rows > li.lp-row` gives each column a `flex` basis, a **15rem** floor on the label measured rather than guessed (the widest station name beside its icon is 206px, *Radio Paradise Mellow Mix*, and the widest tag line under it is 134px, *Classical · Portuguese*), and `flex-wrap: wrap` so a row narrower than both columns puts the sentence **below** the name instead of squeezing it — which is what a phone gets, and what the squeezed layout was doing instead. **The tag itself can no longer be the break point at all**: `ad-free` is written inside `.lp-tag`, which is `white-space: nowrap`, because a hyphen is a break opportunity to a line breaker and telling somebody about ad-free stations in two halves says the opposite of what the tag means. **And it is the tenth sentence in `tests/test_design_laws.py`** — `ALabelColumnKeepsItsRoom` — which holds the row to stating its columns, the label to its floor, the sentence to `min-width: 0`, the tag to `nowrap`, and the template to writing the tag inside it, so the rule cannot become decoration. Three mutations were watched to fail: the label's basis back to `auto` (caught), `wrap` back to `nowrap` (caught), and the `lp-tag` wrapper removed from the template (caught — the first version of that check failed by printing the whole 20KB template, so it asserts with a regex now). **2,507 tests pass, 0 skipped**, and the pass was driven on the *deployed* site before and after rather than on a local build: `/music` at 375px, 480px and 1440px, with the two Pages projects both verified to be serving the same commit as `main`.
- **🧾 The deploy manifest was still asking Render for the extractor's three credentials — a cookie jar, a PO token and visitor data — three commits after the extractor was deleted, and it explained in a paragraph what the code would do with them**: found by walking the surfaces the way a *deploy* reads them rather than the way a test does, and it is the failure the config audit exists for, arriving from the direction nothing was watching. `render.yaml` is the one file an operator answers questions from: it declared `YTDL_COOKIES_B64`, `YTDL_PO_TOKEN` and `YTDL_VISITOR_DATA` as prompts, in a block whose prose still said *the extractor runs anonymously; with them owned by nobody*, when the removal had already taken those seven settings out of `utils/config_audit.py` and left the music section saying the opposite in its own words (*"radio is a URL and ffmpeg"*). **The cost is not clutter, it is a false instruction**: an operator who fills those prompts in has configured nothing, no run of the bot reads them, and the manifest reads as though playing a station still needs a media platform's cookies — which is exactly the belief the last pass deleted the feature to end. The registry already refuses a setting *nothing reads*; the manifest was never held to that rule, because the only check on it asserted the keys that **must** be there and said nothing about the ones that must not. So it is a law now, and it compares against `config_audit.SETTINGS` rather than against a list kept in the test — `test_every_declared_key_is_a_setting_this_bot_reads` — with the two settings music *does* read (`MEOW_FFMPEG`, `STREAM_PROBE_TARGET`) deliberately **not** declared, which the comment says out loud: both exist to override a default that is already right on this host, and a prompt is where a default gets overwritten by somebody answering a question they had no reason to be asked. **A second finding came out of the same sweep, and it was a test that could no longer fail**: `test_a_station_is_never_sent_through_a_proxy` set `YTDL_PROXY` before building the source and then asserted no proxy flag reached ffmpeg — asserting the absence of something nothing could have produced, because the variable it set is deleted and the machinery with it. It is now the half that still bites (the behavioural check, unprompted by a dead name) plus a new law where the removal actually lives: the *player* may not mention a proxy at all and the setting may not be declared again, so a deployment cannot be configured back into needing one. **3 new tests and 1 rewritten**, both laws watched to fail (a `YTDL_PO_TOKEN` line appended to the manifest → 1 failure; a stray `proxy` comment in `utils/player.py` → 1) and the suite re-run after each restore. **2,505 tests pass, 0 skipped.** The rest of the pass was the same walk over the real surfaces with the real `.env` loaded, and it found the sign-in path whole: the passkey opens `/owner`, a wrong one prints *Invalid admin passkey* and leaves the field usable rather than stuck, `_door_target` sends a fresh session through the clickwrap at `/agree?next=/owner` and the console answers 200 on every authenticated route. What a user would still stumble on is *not* found by any test in this repository: the owner host is a Cloudflare worker with its own copy of the route list, so the agreement page is a path that has to be declared in three places at once, and the only thing holding those three together is another test that compares them — the shape that has been wrong twice.
- **🌍 All four languages the gap list could not reach are in the catalogue now — Mandarin, Vietnamese, Persian and Ukrainian — because the first attempt failed on *method*, not on the languages**: the table's own comment named them and said why they were missing (*"a 404, a DNS failure, a 403 and a timeout"*), and three passes later the honest diagnosis is that **the search was the defect**. It guessed at host names; **Radio Suomi** and **Prambors** are both real stations and neither of those addresses exists, and a guess that 404s proves nothing about whether a language can be covered. **So the second attempt did not guess — it asked a public directory for candidates and then verified every one the way everything else in this table is verified**: https, ffmpeg decoding three seconds of it, and ``icy-name`` giving the name that gets written down instead of whatever the directory called it. Where the language could be checked further it was, and this is the evidence that makes the four entries more than directory rows: **Vietnamese Youth Radio's own metadata prints ``Phạm Vỹ - Khó Có Thể``**, **988 FM's prints ``龚芷葳 - 两个人的冰淇淋``**, and Radio Donya's prints a Persian schedule — the station's own words, in the station's own script, which is as close to *"the words are in this language"* as a probe that cannot listen can get. **The four**: Radio ROKS (Kyiv rock, 320 kbps MP3), Radio Donya (Persian pop from the diaspora), Vietnamese Youth Radio, and **988 FM** (Malaysia's Mandarin pop station) — one flagship per language, named for what each stream says it is. `online.radioroks.ua` joins `STREAM_HOSTS` because its path is an icecast mount rather than a file, which is the same defect the last pass found twice; the other three are already covered by an extension (`.aac`) or by a host the list already has. **The catalogue is 146 stations over 29 languages** — the floor in the radio suite moves 25 → 29 with it, and a new test names the four so a language cannot leave the list without something failing. **2,502 tests pass, 0 skipped**, and the public page's own count moved with it (``146 in 29 languages``).
- **📻 The `ad-free` label now *does* something — `/music ads` gives a server three answers about stations that run commercials, and the picker marks the ones that don't**: the previous pass made *no advertising* a tag, a search and a number, and a label a member has to think about is half a feature. **`/music ads` is the setting that acts on it, with three answers rather than a switch**, because *no ads* is two different requests: `any` (the default — a setting that changes what plays must not arrive switched on), `prefer` (*when nobody names a station, pick one that carries none*), and `only` (*never start one that does, even if a member names it*). **`only` is the mode that has to say no to a person**, and so the mode a server has to opt into; when it refuses, the card names the station, the setting and the command that changes it — 28 of 142 stations qualify, and a server that switches to `only` and finds the catalogue unusable should find that out from the card rather than from a member complaining. **The memory behaves differently in the two acting modes on purpose**: `/music radio` with no argument replays whatever this server last chose, and under `only` a remembered station that carries advertising is set aside (a *rule* is about the server, not about who typed first), while under `prefer` it is still played (a *preference* is not a rule, and the server chose that station deliberately). The same distinction governs the pick: `prefer` and `only` both draw from the ad-free stations when any exist, and `prefer` falls back to the whole catalogue while `only` cannot. **The storage is deliberately not the row it governs.** The mode lives in `guild_settings` under `music_radio_ads` rather than in `music_radio`, because that table's one row *is* what this server last played and is rewritten on every start — a preference sharing a row with the thing it controls would be overwritten by the very feature it was set for. It is read through `db.get_radio_ads`, which turns anything it does not recognise back into `any`, so a hand-edited or restored database can never silence radio, and written through `db.set_radio_ads`, which refuses a value that is not one of the three at the door rather than storing a fourth meaning nobody reads. **And the claim shows up where the choice is made**: an ad-free station's autocomplete label ends `· ad-free`, in the positive direction only — a station whose site did not say is left unmarked rather than marked *ads*, which would be this bot asserting something it never read. The audience is declared `manage_guild` and not the owner, with the difference written down in the table: `/music 247` keeps the bot in a channel indefinitely and is a resource decision the owner makes, while this changes what a member hears and belongs to whoever configures the bot — and it can refuse a member's own station, which is why it is not open to everyone. **14 new tests**, four of them watched to fail: the refusal removed → 2 failures, the memory rule removed → 1, `prefer` un-biased → 11, the stored value trusted verbatim → 1. The registry, the public page and `docs/discord-directory.md` describe the new setting — the page's row list is generated from the registry, so `/music ads` appeared there without being written twice. **2,502 tests pass, 0 skipped.**
- **📻 Stations play commercials and the catalogue had no way to say which ones don't — `ad-free` is now a tag, a search and a number, applied only where a station's own site makes the claim**: *"the stations play commercials"*, which is the one complaint about radio that code cannot patch. A station URL is a stream somebody else programs; there is no filter that removes an ad break from a live MP3 without decoding and re-encoding the music around it, and a bot that did that would be worse than the ad. **So the fix is a label over the catalogue rather than a filter over the audio** — and, because a label nobody can check is a promise the bot cannot keep, the same rule the URL list is held to: the station's own words. `RADIO_TAGS` gains `ad-free` ("no advertising — the station's own site says so") and it is applied to exactly the **28 entries whose sites said it**: every SomaFM channel, because SomaFM's support page says *"a commercial-free, listener supported radio station … We don't run ads because that would mean giving up our independence"* — the claim is about the operator rather than the channel, so all twenty-seven carry it — and WFMU, whose about page says *"a listener-supported, non-commercial radio station"*. **The broadcasters most likely to qualify are deliberately left untagged, and that is the point of the pass.** Yle, NRK, DR, Sveriges Radio, RTP, NPO and Radio France are all funded in ways that do not want advertising, and *none of their own pages stated it* in words this pass could read; KEXP's page says *listener-supported* and no more, which a station can say while still running sponsor spots. The table has one documented case of a confident directory label being wrong — the *baroque* entry that was a Bee Gees station — so silence is the honest entry for a station whose site did not say. **Nobody types the tag, so the words they do type are mapped to it**: `ad-free` flattens to `adfree`, and a search for *no ads* is the two words *no* and *ads*, neither of which is in the tag — so `ad`, `ads`, `advert`, `adverts`, `advertising`, `commercial` and `commercials` all reach it through the synonym table that exists for exactly this. **And it is on the surfaces rather than only in the data**: the index prints `28 carry no advertising` with the search that finds them, the station card prints tags already so an ad-free station says so as it starts, and the public `/music` page marks the row (`Chill · ad-free`) — the one fact a visitor choosing something to leave on wants before it starts rather than during. Four new tests, all watched to fail: synonyms removed → 2 failures, a commercial station tagged → 1, the index line emptied → 1. **2,488 tests pass, 0 skipped.**
- **🌍 The radio could play twenty-five languages and could not *say* so anywhere: `/music stations` indexed genres and never languages, and 94 of the 142 stations carried no language tag with nothing on any surface separating *no words* from *not established*** — *"Radio Doesn't have lanauge"*, and the complaint was structural rather than about the table. The table has carried `lang` since it grew past six stations and the *search* has understood it since the pass that added one flagship per language — but the **index** did not, and the index is the only place a member can find out what is there: `/music stations` with no word printed genres and their counts, so somebody who does not read this bot's language had one question to ask (*is my language in it?*) and no way to ask it except by guessing a language and hoping the search box had been given that word. It now prints **both axes**: the languages first, alphabetically with counts (that list is a lookup, and a lookup is scanned), then the genres most-populated first (that list is a browse), under one line that says how wide the table is — **142 stations · 25 languages · 54 genres**. **The blanks were the other half, and counting them as one bucket was the lie.** A blank `lang` was reported as *"no language"*, which is true of the catalogue's ambient and electronic entries and false of the rest: 41 of the blanks carry the catalogue's own `instrumental` tag, which means there are no words to be in a language, and the other 53 play words in whatever the music is in — a thing this table has **not established** rather than one it has decided. `catalogue_index()` now returns those apart (`wordless`, `unlabelled`), the index says both sentences instead of one that is true of half the rows, and a test holds the split to the tags so it cannot drift back into a bucket. **And language is on every surface that lists a station now rather than only in the card that plays one.** The public `/music` page passed three keys of each entry and rendered a name and a sentence, which is the shape that cannot answer the question the page is read for; it passes the whole entry, each row now reads `Eclectic · Finnish`, and the panel head says *142 in 25 languages*. The console's music panel — the surface an administrator actually watches — gained a **Catalogue** row (`142 stations · 25 languages · 41 wordless · 53 with no language established`), read from the cog so it cannot drift from what plays, with `null` as its own drawn state when the cog cannot be read. **Two of the six languages the previous pass named as gaps closed, because the first pass guessed at host names and the second asked each broadcaster's own platform**: Finnish is **Yle Radio 1** (`icecast.live.yle.fi`, whose path ends in `icecast.audio` — a server's word rather than an extension, so the *host* is declared in `STREAM_HOSTS`) and Indonesian is **Trax Streaming** (Radiojar's `stream.radiojar.com`, named for what its own `icy-name` says rather than for what a directory calls it) — each opened with an HTTP GET and **then decoded by the real ffmpeg** before either was written down, and the gap note now names the four that are still open (Mandarin, Vietnamese, Farsi, Ukrainian) with the shape of their refusal. **Eight new tests** (2 rewritten, 5 in the radio suite, 2 for the console payload, a class for the public page), and every one was watched to fail: stripping the language off a row failed 4 subtests, emptying the index's language line failed 21, and collapsing the two blank counts into one failed 2. **2,484 tests pass, 0 skipped**, `tsc --noEmit` clean, bundle re-stamped `6fdf963397f7`.
- **📻 `yt-dlp` is gone and radio is the whole of playback — `/play` and the five commands that were only ever a front end for it are deleted, the resolver is archived intact, and the one thing the extractor was *for* (answering "the music is short") is now enforced where it actually applies**: *"remove the music and keep radio only"*, carried out as the removal it is rather than as a frozen copy of a feature that cannot work. **The argument is the one this project has been making for three passes and finally acted on.** `/play` turned words into one specific recording through `yt-dlp`, a ladder of player clients, three providers, a second-provider rescue and a Spotify half — and every one of those paths ended at a *media platform*, where a datacenter address is a robot, which is why there were cookies (`YTDL_COOKIES`, `YTDL_COOKIES_B64`), a PO token (`YTDL_PO_TOKEN`, `YTDL_VISITOR_DATA`), a provider order (`YTDL_SOURCE_ORDER`) and an outbound proxy (`YTDL_PROXY`) to answer it. Six settings and a client ladder were maintenance with an expiry date; a station URL is a direct stream that asks nobody anything, which is why radio was always the thing that worked and is now the thing that exists. **`/play`, `/music songinfo`, `/music lyrics`, `/music seek`, `/music loop` and `/music shuffle` are deleted with their helpers** (`_resolve_in_executor`, `_enqueue_and_play`, `TrackChoiceView`, `_itunes`, `_lyrics`, `_unplayable_hint`, and the `blocked`/`rescued` fault kinds and cards), `yt-dlp` is out of `requirements.txt`, and the seven `YTDL_*` settings are out of `utils/config_audit.py` — replaced by one, `STREAM_PROBE_TARGET`, which names a *station*. **And `imageio-ffmpeg` stays, deliberately**: the request named it as a thing to drop and the opposite is true — ffmpeg is what decodes a station's MP3/AAC and encodes Opus for Discord, so removing it would silence the very feature being kept. **The archive is whole, and it is four files**: `deleted_features/utils/media_source.py` is the module as it stood, and the live one is now the stream half and nothing else — `Track` and the probe (`Probe`, `probe_target`, `self_check`) — because `Stream` was what a *provider* handed back and the proxy helper was what carried a *signed* fetch, and there is neither a provider nor a signature left to carry. With it: `tests/test_music_matching.py` (87 tests), `tests/test_music_playback.py` (the resolver half of the player's suite: rescue, seek, loop, the fetch proxy, and the `/play` command-surface assertions) and a new `test_radio_refusals.py` — the two classes that lived in `tests/test_radio.py`, kept because *"nothing is wrong with your search"* versus *"the platform refused this host"* is a distinction any future resolver will have to make again. Restoring one means moving those files and putting `yt-dlp` back; `yt-dlp` is already in `requirements-archived.txt`.
- **🔇 The check that produced "the music is short" now covers the shape radio actually has — a station that closes the connection cleanly, which ffmpeg reports as a *finished* stream**: the guard compared how long a source played against the length it declared, and a station declares no length (`duration=0`), so the whole comparison was unreachable on the day it started mattering most. A **live** source has no end, so the rule is now about what ended rather than only about how long it ran: a live source that reports itself finished **was** cut, and a source that declares a length and stops far short of it was cut too. The card says which of the two it was (`the station went quiet after **4:12** — a live stream has no end, so that is a dropped connection rather than a finished song`), because "it stopped" and "the connection dropped" send somebody to different places, and the archived `-err_detect ignore_err` is still nowhere near the command line. The player itself lost only what had no user left — the resolve/rescue block, `seek`, the loop branches, the `-ss` prefix and the proxy argument — and kept the generation counter, the timers, the three source paths and the 24/7 seat, so the parts of it that were measured decisions are untouched.
- **📡 The weekly media check's third target stopped being an extractor and became a *stream*, which changed what it can prove — and immediately found a duplicate probe nobody had**: the target used to be "can `yt-dlp` still resolve a recording" and is now "can this host open a station at all" (`media_source.self_check` opens `STREAM_PROBE_TARGET`, SomaFM's Groove Salad by default, all the way to a content type and a first byte). It is the one target that is not a *link*: every station URL in the catalogue can answer while the host has no route to the outside world, and no URL finding can see the difference. **The rename went all the way through** — `extractor_state()` is `stream_state()`, the console's music card reads `music.stream` instead of `music.extractor`, and the `climbing` status and the `noted` bucket are gone with the client ladder they described, because a third bucket that nothing can fill is a status a reader has to learn for nothing. **And the probe station is *in the catalogue*, so the scan found the same URL in `cogs/music.py` and the pass asked about it twice** — 151 probes over 150 addresses, caught by the catalogue's own "a station is never probed twice" test on the first run: `media_health.targets()` now lets a declared target claim its URL (the declared name wins, because `stream:radio` says *what* broke where `cogs/music.py:NNN` only says where it is written), and the count is honest again. **`/music doctor` reports the same question** — what ffmpeg and libopus are, how many stations there are, and whether a real one opens — and `keep_alive`'s voice-debug payload carries it as `stream`, while the operator's music remedies lost the two kinds that can no longer be raised.
- **🎛️ Every surface that described the old feature now describes this one, and the ones that would have lied were rebuilt rather than edited around**: the console's music card (row renamed to *Stations*, the *on a fallback client* pill replaced by *no route out*, `player.identity` and `player.loop` dropped from the payload type and the row), `cogs/help.py`'s music category and home bullet, the feature registry in `utils/features.py` (**one root, twelve commands**, `gate_commands=("music",)`), the five permission declarations that no longer have a command, `utils/operator_log.py`'s remedies, the preview harness's fake payload, the public `templates/music.html` (**its three-complaints section, its radio band, its "what is said out loud" panel, its command band and its limits band all rewritten** — *"It will not play a song by name"* is now a limit stated on purpose, next to the route-gate row in `docs/architecture.md` and `docs/discord-directory.md`'s listing copy at *15 systems and 95 slash commands*), and `templates/music.html`'s meta description with them. The console was rebuilt and re-stamped `4ef31fcb5458`.
- **🌍 The radio catalogue had become a wide catalogue of *genres* and a narrow one of *languages* — thirteen languages are now in the table that were not, one well-known station each, and every one of them was fetched before it was written down**: *"add more lanauge of music like multiple lanauge radio and stuff"*, after the pass that took the catalogue to 122 stations. That pass grew it by genre, which is the axis a *browse* is built on — and it left the axis a **search** is built on half-done: somebody whose language was not French, German, Japanese, Korean, Hindi, Turkish or Arabic could type their own words into `/music stations` and be told no, on a bot whose radio exists precisely to be the thing that always plays. **So this pass is one flagship per language, and the tag is the entry rather than a detail on it.** Spanish (LOS40), Italian (Rai Radio 3), Portuguese (Antena 1 and 2), Russian (Nashe Radio), Polish (RMF FM), Dutch (NPO Radio 1 and 2), Norwegian (NRK P1), Danish (DR P1), Swedish (Sveriges Radio P1), Czech (ČRo Radiožurnál), Hungarian (Klubrádió), Romanian (Radio România Actualități), Greek (ERT Kosmos), Hebrew (Galei Tzahal), Thai (Cool Fahrenheit) and a second Japanese one (J1 HITS) — **18 streams, 13 languages new to the catalogue**, which now carries 23. **The rule that governs this table was followed literally: every URL answered an ordinary GET with audio before it was written down** (26 candidates were probed, 18 survived), and the failures are named in the table's own comment rather than left to be rediscovered — **Mandarin, Indonesian, Vietnamese, Farsi, Finnish and Ukrainian** all have broadcasters and none of the addresses this host could reach answered with audio. A language with no station is a gap in the table, not a language this bot has decided against. **Two defects in the *checking* came out of it, both of them the same shape as the one this table's history is made of.** `tests/test_radio.py` holds the catalogue to *every station being in the weekly probe*, and it named **ten of the eighteen** as unprobed: a stream with no file extension is a host and a word, so it has to be **declared** in `media_health.STREAM_HOSTS` — which is exactly what that list is for, and it now carries nine more entries, with the ports written in where they are part of the host (`jenny.torontocast.com:2000`, `stream4.srr.ro:8443`) and the *domain* declared where the subdomain rotates (`rmfstream.pl`, which RMF hands out as `rs6-krk2`, `rs7-…`). And the tenth was a genuinely wrong line of code: **`STREAM_HOSTS`'s own note has said *"the extension test saw the 31 ending in `.mp3`/`.aac`"* since the day it was written, and `MEDIA_EXTENSIONS` did not contain `.aac`** — so an AAC station was never recognised as media at all, and the weekly check would have gone on not asking about it forever. The prose and the code disagreed and the code won quietly. Both halves are fixed, and the catalogue's floors moved with them: **languages `>= 8` is now `>= 20`** (a floor, not a target — it is what stops the table shrinking back to the languages its maintainer happens to speak), plus a new test that the tag and the *ranking* agree, because a `lang` the search cannot match is a station that exists and cannot be found, which is the same experience as one that is not there. 2565 tests pass (**1 new, 2 rewritten**), 0 skipped, 450 subtests in the radio suite alone.
- **🖱️ Two cursor layers that painted nothing were built and positioned sixty times a second on every page of the bot — the centre dot and the trailing ring are gone, and the spring they carried now drives the layers that are actually drawn**: the last pass left them as a deliberate open question (*"decide whether to make them draw or remove them"*), so this one counted what they cost rather than what they were for. **They could not draw, and that is not a matter of taste.** `Cursor.tsx` and `motion.js` each built the pair under the chosen entity and pinned it to the pointer every frame; `styles.css` and `meow.css` each hid both in every style the picker offers, and **neither sheet ever gave either one a `width`, a `height`, a `border` or a `background`** — so even un-hidden they were 0×0 boxes, and the two nodes plus two writes per frame bought exactly zero pixels. The judgement was already written in the sheet: `cursor-style-sparkle`'s own header says *"zero center dot, zero ring"*, and a second mark on the point is the *multiple cursors* this layer exists to answer rather than to make. **What was load-bearing was kept.** The ring's spring is what the glow, the blob, the radar and the vortex trail on in both scripts, and it is what the loop's own settling test measures, so the spring stays and only the four DOM writes and the two nodes go — `ringX`/`ringY` became dead state in `motion.js` and were removed with it, `STRETCH`/`STRETCH_MAX` became dead constants in `Cursor.tsx` (the blob measures its own squash from real velocity, which is the reaction they were standing in for), and the comments in four files that described the layer as *"the fallback"* now say what is there instead. **Verified on both served pages rather than in the source**: `document.querySelectorAll` finds zero of either node, and all five drawn styles put their entity where the pointer is — sparkle's swarm around `(640, 420)`, the blade, the radar's four brackets, the blob and the vortex each centred on `(700, 460)`, with `system` hiding the layer entirely. Two tests that pinned the pair were rewritten rather than deleted: `tests/test_console.py` now asserts the names are absent from *both* the sheet and the script (it used to assert the ring was there, on a docstring claiming a hairline ring was what a reader sees), and `ThePointerIsMovedByOneThing`'s element list drops the two names with the reason stated in its docstring — a law about where an element is positioned has nothing to say about an element that is not carried — while gaining a check that neither name can come back into `motion.js`. **2,564 tests pass, 0 skipped**, `tsc --noEmit` clean, bundle re-stamped `401ec90bebb0`.
- **🖱️ The cursor was never at the pointer — every one of the six styles sat spinning in the top-left corner of the window, and on the public pages that meant a sheet that says `cursor: none` with nothing on it at all**: *"fix the whole cursor thing like multiple cursor are bugged"*, and the plural was the clue. Measured on the two served pages rather than reasoned about: with the pointer at **(600, 400)** the default sparkle swarm's particles were at **(-9, -9)** and the blade at **(0, 1)** — six stars twinkling in the corner of the viewport while the arrow moved, which is exactly what *multiple cursors* looks like — and on the dashboard, whose own rule is `cursor: none`, the visitor had **no cursor anywhere on the page**. **The cause is two parties writing one property.** Both `Cursor.tsx` and `motion.js` positioned the layer with `style.transform = translate3d(x, y, …)` while both sheets animate `transform` — the twinkle, the sword's bob, the slash — and an animation outranks an inline declaration, so it did not *compose* with the position, it **replaced** it. **So the split is now by property and it is stated where each half is written: the script owns `translate`, the sheet owns `transform`.** They compose in that order, around the element's own origin, and neither can cancel the other. All eight positioned elements were converted, and the same collision had quietly killed **every** `.is-link` and `.is-down` rule that states a `transform` — and two of them had never been drawn even once, because the element's *own* animation outranked them too: the sparkle's click starburst and the sword's charge stance now cancel their animation (`animation: none`) instead of losing to it. **Two more ways the cursor could be somewhere other than the pointer came out of the same reading.** The console's swarm was released from **(-100, -100)** on the first move — `motion.js` has always snapped the particles to the pointer, the console's copy of that loop did not — and both loops stopped the moment the *ring* settled, which is a handful of frames, while the particles ease in at a lag of 0.14–0.34 per frame: a fast flick left the six of them strung out along the path. Both loops now wait for the swarm. **And the states the two scripts write are the states the two sheets now answer**: `is-text` has been put on the host by both scripts since the six styles were added and was ruled by neither sheet (the layer now steps back over a field, `filter: brightness(0.7) saturate(0.6)` — `filter` rather than `opacity`, because the console's script reveals the layer by writing `opacity` inline and a second mechanism over one property is the defect this whole pass is about), while `cursor--link` was a second name for `is-link` that no selector anywhere answered and is gone. **Three separate lists had to agree for the sign-in to work at all, and each of them was missing the same path.** `_door_target` sends every freshly signed-in session to `/agree?next=…` before anything else — the clickwrap is at the door by design — and that path is a `Location` header, so it appears in no template and no `.ts`/`.tsx` file: on the owner host the worker did not list it, and its answer to a path it does not recognise is a 301 to the public site, **so the operator entered the right passkey and landed signed out on another host at a page offering no passkey field**, which is what *"pass entre is shit not taking password"* was; on the public host the worker's `DYNAMIC_PREFIXES` did not list it **and neither did `_routes.json`**, which is the gate Cloudflare reads to decide whether the worker runs at all — so the agreement 404'd for every member on a host that had been taught to proxy it and never got the chance. Both lists are fixed and both sites rebuilt, and the two are now compared to each other by test rather than trusted. **The operator's sign-in page could not be scrolled on a phone**, which is the third way that form wastes a correct passkey: measured at 360x380 — a small phone with the keyboard up — the card is 507px tall, the Unlock button ended at 381px, one pixel below the fold, and the way back to the public site was off the page entirely, with a maximum scroll position of **0** against a 555px document because the template's own `body { overflow: hidden }` forbade scrolling. `overflow-x: hidden` is what the guard against the tilted card always wanted, the height is now `100dvh` with a `vh` fallback, and the dead `font-size: 15px` on the passkey field is gone — `input[type="password"] { font-size: var(--text-body) }` in the shared sheet is more specific and already won, and 16px is also the floor below which iOS zooms the whole page when a field takes focus. **And the theme is one choice on every surface now**: the console remembered it as `meow-console-theme` while `static/theme.js` — loaded in `<head>` by every page the bot serves — reads `meow-theme`, so a theme picked in the console was not the theme of the page you opened next, and the console's old name is still *read* so nobody's existing choice is reset. **All of it is law now**: `tests/test_design_laws.py` gains its ninth sentence, `ThePointerIsMovedByOneThing` — no sheet may write `translate` on an element the pointer positions, no keyframe it applies may animate it, the two scripts must position with `translate` and never with `transform`, the swarm must start where the pointer does and both loops must wait for it, and a state rule may not restate a property its element's animation owns without saying what happens to that animation — with the elements named and the list checked against both scripts so a rename cannot leave it watching nothing. Nine mutations were watched to fail, including *both* of my own first drafts: a law that read `0%   { transform` as a property name (two checks green while the defect was written straight back in) and a check that tested the state modifier on the *subject* when it lives in the first compound. 13 new tests (**2,564 total, 0 skipped**), `tsc --noEmit` clean, bundle re-stamped `7c55e880afe8`, and every style and state driven in a browser on the served pages before and after — sparkle, sword, radar, blob and vortex each on the pointer, the starburst, the charge stance and the `is-text` step all reading their computed values.
- **🔗 A class name the stylesheet had never been told about, in both directions — the Overview's two dials sat one under the other in a card with half its width empty, and the Access page's caveats printed at body size and full brightness — and neither was visible in a test that reads the server**: *"check for bug and fix and ship them"*, one pass after the five undefined `var()`s, which is the same defect one level up: there a *token* was read and never declared, here a *class* is written and never ruled. **The sweep is what found the second one.** Every class name the console writes — literals, the strings inside `className={…}` expressions, the head of a template before its `${…}`, and the classes the cursor script sets on `<html>` — against every class selector `console/src/styles.css` rules, with comments stripped first: **six names were written and ruled nowhere**, and each was one of two shapes. **A name whose rule was simply missing.** `card__foot` is written three times on the Access page to say what qualifies a card — *"this host cannot write to the ledger"*, *"the history these counts carry is no longer than uptime"*, *"without the signature, one person's five wrong passkeys close the door for everybody"* — and with no rule it was 16px at full brightness, i.e. exactly the size and weight of the claim it was qualifying. It is now what the sheet already had a vocabulary for: `--dim`, `--type-meta`, the scale's own rhythm, and a hairline to set it off. **A name borrowed from a component that no longer renders.** The Overview's dials carried `.telemetry-row` — the wrapper of a *column* of label/value/bar rows, five of whose six rules nothing renders any more — and the markup stated the layout it actually wanted in an inline style that listed `flex`, `gap` and `flex-wrap` but never `flex-direction`, so the column rule stood and **the two dials stacked vertically in a four-column card with nothing in the right half of it**: `flex-wrap: wrap` was in that style, which only means anything in a row, and the sheet won the argument it never heard. The row is now `.gauges` — its own rule, in the sheet, a wrapping row — the inline style is gone, and the dead `telemetry-row` family went with it. **Three more names said nothing at all and are gone rather than answered**: `toolbar--bare` (a modifier that strips chrome the base `.toolbar` never had), `cmds__row is-off` (a row state nothing drew, asserting *off* one line above cells that report on per server), and `question__where` (an intent to set the server apart from the timestamp that was never written). `.live-pulse__line` went the other way and got the rule it deserved — round caps and joins on a 2px trace, the cap `.gauge__val` already uses. And the Economy page's 12px came out of the markup and into the sheet as `.toolbar + .toolbar`, since two stacked toolbars are a spacing decision the stylesheet should own. **The fourth case was a flag set for nobody, and the honest fix turned out to be deleting the flag.** `Cursor.tsx` and `theme.ts` were putting `has-meow-cursor` on `<html>` so a sheet could hide the native pointer — the rule the *dashboard's* sheet answers with `cursor: none !important` — and this console had decided the opposite: its ring is a **companion** to the pointer, a decision `tests/test_console.py` already pins by refusing `cursor: none` anywhere in the sheet. Adding the dashboard's rule would have been this pass overruling a tested decision, so the class is no longer set: a promise to a rule that deliberately does not exist reads as *the pointer is hidden somewhere else*. **Every one of these was checked against the *served* page before and after**: the dials read `flex-direction: column` with `y` equal for both children and now sit side by side at the same `y`, the caveats read `rgb(243,243,246)`/16px and now `rgb(155,155,168)`/13px under a hairline, the stacked toolbar reads `margin-top: 12px` from the sheet with no `style` attribute anywhere in the console. **And it is a law now — the eighth sentence, `MarkupAndSheetAgree`** — with the two allowances stated in its docstring rather than left to be discovered: a class name that arrives through a prop (`className={className}`, how `Link` forwards one) is skipped, and a name cut short where an interpolation begins (`pill--`, `card--span-`) is checked as a *prefix* some rule has to complete, with that allowance bounded at the five places it is real so widening it is a decision rather than a drift. Six mutations were watched to fail: `toolbar--bare` back in the markup, the `card__foot` rule deleted, `cmds__row` restored, a literal `style={{ marginTop: "12px" }}` put back, a class name declared in one file and removed from the sheet, and `classList.add("has-meow-cursor")` restored in `theme.ts`. 3 new tests (2,551 total, 0 skipped), `tsc --noEmit` clean, bundle re-stamped `1b5f97bd090b`.
- **🎨 The themes in this project are deliberately *partial* — the base block carries the palette and each theme redefines the handful of values that make it a theme — and nothing was checking the one thing that arrangement depends on**: a theme may override a name, and it must never be the *only* place that name exists. `:root, [data-theme="midnight"]` declares fifty names in the console and the eight themes after it override thirteen each; the public sheet's base declares forty-nine and its eight themes override six (the hue, and nothing else); the hub's declares forty-five and its two light blocks override twenty-two. **Every one of those overrides is a name the base already carries, which is exactly what makes the arrangement safe** — and a single name declared inside a theme block and nowhere else would break that theme's siblings in the silent way this file keeps rediscovering: every other theme drops the declaration and paints nothing, while a sweep for *undeclared* names passes, because the name is declared somewhere. **So the law is one direction — (names a theme declares) ⊆ (names its base declares)** — checked on all three sheets rather than on the console alone. The second half is the hub's own check made general: a sheet's themes must declare *the same* names as each other, so a theme cannot quietly drop an override and render that token in the base's colours. **Which was written first as "at least what the others carry" and could never fail** — an intersection of the theme sets is by definition a subset of every one of them, so the theme that *drops* an override shrinks the intersection and passes it: deleting `--warn` from the forest theme sailed through, the mutation caught it, and the check is equality now. Four mutations were watched to fail — a name declared only inside one theme, in the console and in the public sheet; an override dropped from one theme; and the hub's media-query twin losing a token its chosen-light block has, which `tests/test_hub.py` already pinned for that pair and which is where this law came from. Component-local properties (`.btn { --btn-text: … }`, the landing page's `--lp-*`, the shell's own measurements) and theme-scoped descendant rules (``[data-theme="light"] .aurora``) are deliberately out of scope and named as such in the docstring: they declare next to their own reads, and the undeclared-name sweep is what covers them. 3 new tests (2,548 total, 0 skipped).
- **🧭 `static/dashboard.css` is 1,999 lines that declare three custom properties and read twenty-seven from a file they never mention — which is correct, and which nothing was checking**: the dashboard's layout sheet is loaded *after* `static/meow.css` on every page that has one, and the split is stated in both files' own headers — **the palette is shared, the layout is the page's** — so neither file means anything on its own, and the only thing standing behind each of those twenty-seven names was that nobody had renamed one yet. It is the silence the console's five undefined tokens lived in, one level up: there the sheet was missing its own declarations, and here the missing ones are in a *different file*, which is exactly what a sweep over "the stylesheet" cannot see. **So the law is about the page, and it walks templates rather than files**: every linked sheet, every inline `<style>`, every inline script and every `<script src>`, in the order the browser gets them — and a `var()` with no fallback has to be declared by something that had already arrived when that sheet did, an earlier sheet, an earlier inline block, or a `setProperty()` in a script. Two details keep the check honest rather than merely green: inline JavaScript is read as *setters* and not as text, because a `--name:` inside JS is a string rather than a declaration; and `var(--x, …)` is its own definition, which is how the runtime-set cursor properties satisfy it. **All 28 templates pass, and the interesting half is the four-line assertion that it looked at the right pair** — `/static/meow.css` before `/static/dashboard.css` in the same document, and more than twenty names the dashboard sheet reads and does not declare — so the day somebody inlines that palette, the law says so instead of passing vacuously. Both directions were watched to fail: a read added to `dashboard.css`, and `--border` deleted from `meow.css`, which is the rename in the *other* file that no test could have caught before. The self-contained half of the same law now sweeps two sheets rather than one — `hub/static/hub.css` is generated into pages by `scripts/build_hub.py` and carries its own palette, so it answers to the same sentence (watched to fail the same way). 2 new tests (2,545 total, 0 skipped).
- **🧩 Five custom properties the owner console's stylesheet has never defined — one of them in an SVG `stroke`, where an undefined `var()` means the element is not painted at all — so the Access page's session rows had no background and body-sized metadata, and the Overview's Fleet Reach gauge was an empty ring with "40%" written in the middle of it**: found by reading the *served* pages rather than the source, in the pass that added the Questions page: `--muted`, `--radius-2`, `--surface-2` and `--type-small` are Discord's palette names, carried in with the markup this console replaced, and `--pill-good-text` is a token this sheet never had. **The failure mode is the quiet one, and that is the whole reason nothing in 37 commits since could see it**: `color: var(--muted)` is valid CSS that paints the inherited colour, `font-size: var(--type-small)` is valid CSS that changes nothing, and an undefined name in a `stroke` resolves to `none` — so nothing errored, nothing was logged, and the diff that added each one looked exactly like the diff that works. It was visible only by opening the page: the session list was seven flat rows whose metadata matched its titles in size and brightness, and the second gauge on the Overview drew its track and its percentage with no arc between them. **The fix is the house tokens rather than four new ones**: `--dim` for the two text colours, `--type-meta` for the three type sizes, `--radius-sm` plus the panel-2 glass row the Live page's `.alone__row` already uses, and `--good` for the gauge, which is the colour the Good pill beside it is drawn with — verified in the browser on both pages by reading computed styles before and after (`stroke: none` → `rgb(57, 217, 138)`; `rgba(0, 0, 0, 0)`, `0px`, `16px` → the panel row, `10px`, `13px`). **And it is a law now, because it is the one class of defect no other check can see**: `tests/test_design_laws.py` gains a sixth sentence — *nothing reads a token that does not exist* — as `EveryTokenIsDefined`, which walks every `var()` in the console's sources that carries no fallback and fails on a name the sheet does not declare and `theme.ts` does not `setProperty`. Allowed by definition rather than by exception: the cursor's four are set at runtime *and* always read with a fallback, which is what makes them legitimate. Both mutations were watched to fail — `var(--muted)` put back in one rule, `--pill-good-text` put back in the gauge. 3 new tests (2,543 total, 0 skipped), `tsc --noEmit` clean, bundle re-stamped `c09d09cf81ec`.
- **❓ The questions can now be answered from the website, on the one screen allowed to show who asked — and the console's toast was caught printing Discord's markdown into HTML**: *"the qna cmd isn't there and the site is still fucked look look ui ux … no way to reply to the questions"*, read off a screenshot of the owner console. **The reply path existed and was reachable only from Discord.** `/qna ask` posts the question with nobody's name on it, the owner gets a DM naming the asker with an Answer button, the answer is DMed and never posted — all of that worked, and all of it required an operator to answer a question from a phone card that scrolls away, or to type `/qna reply question:<id>`. The operator was looking at the console when they said there was no way to reply, so the console is where the way now is: a **Questions** page, fourth under *Work*, reading the same table the DM is built from. **The asymmetry is the feature, so the page says it out loud in three places**: the channel sees `anon 4F2A` and the question, the operator sees that *and* `RAJ 1542296506290143349` beside it, and the route is gated by `_require_operator_api` like every other console route — asserted on the *body*, not on the status, because a 403 that still handed back a member's name would pass a status-code test. **A question the channel never saw is marked as such**, with the webhook's own reason (`the webhook did not accept it (HTTPError)`), because "waiting for you" and "the relay refused it" are different findings and only one of them is answered by typing a reply. **And a box that could not be read is never reported as empty** — the reading carries its own `ok`/`because` and the page prints *"the questions could not be read"* above the list, since "nothing is waiting" is the one sentence an operator acts on by doing nothing. **Which the page then said anyway.** Found by reading the built page against its own band: the notice was drawn, and the card underneath it still read **"Nothing is waiting"** with **"0 open · 0 answered"** beside it — the same sentence in a smaller font, printed from a payload whose totals are deliberately zeros that mean *unknown*. The unread branch now replaces the list *and* the shell around it — the heading, the hint, and the counts drawn as the em dash the Access page already uses for a figure nobody took — and `tests/test_console.py` pins it by reading the page's own source, because Python cannot see which branch a `.tsx` takes and the server-side test can only prove the payload said *unknown*, never that the page believed it. **The reading is synchronous on purpose** (`qna.owner_inbox_sync`): a page that could only be drawn while the bot's event loop was running would show nothing during exactly the deploys and restarts an operator opens it to check on. **The delivery is not reimplemented, it is shared.** `cogs.qna.deliver_answer(client, id, answer)` was extracted out of the button's callback, and the route calls it on the bot's own loop: the order inside it — *claim the question, then DM, then put it back if Discord refuses the DM* — is what stops an asker being DMed twice and what stops an answer being lost, so a second copy for the web would have been a second place for that order to be wrong. The console and the Answer button now take one code path, and the sentence that reports the outcome is built once too: `answer_sentence(result)` differs between the surfaces by **two** things and no more — the phrase for where a question goes back to (`/qna inbox` vs *this list*) and whether the asker's name is `**bold**`, the second one added after **the browser showed the first version printing the asterisks**: `✅ Sent to **Meow** as a DM` is the right sentence on a Discord card and four stray characters in an HTML toast where nothing renders markdown. `sent: false` with a sentence is a *working* request (an empty box, a question answered in another tab, a DM Discord refused) and a refusal that changed nothing is reported as one rather than as a failure; only "this process has no bot in it" is a 503, because nothing was attempted. **Two defects came out of building it, both the harness's and both the kind that hide a working feature**: `_PreviewBot.get_user` returned `fetch_user`'s *coroutine* instead of a member — Discord's `get_user` is synchronous — so every reply in a preview failed with `coroutine.send() takes no keyword arguments`, a refusal that looked exactly like Discord's and was entirely the stand-in's; and the seeded questions stored each member's `sub` (`@raj`) as the name where the harness's `label` (`RAJ`) is what the owner's DM renders. **31 new tests** (2,540 total, 0 skipped): the operator's reading (names and counts from the table, unknown-rather-than-empty, a table a restored backup forgot), the shared delivery driven the way the route drives it (two surfaces cannot DM twice), the four route refusals — no bot, no id, no CSRF token, a session scoped to one server — both payload schemas against the server's own answer, and the harness seeding the real table once and being answered through. The two new routes were added to the console's `CONSOLE_ROUTES`/`CONSOLE_WRITE_ROUTES` tuples — the list that exists precisely so a new console route cannot slip past the access tests. `tsc --noEmit` clean, bundle re-stamped `ae0cbec16709`, and the page driven in a browser: three questions read with their names, one answered with the toast reading *"✅ Sent to RAJ as a DM, and anon 0001 is closed. It was not posted anywhere."*, the row leaving the list and the tally moving to *2 open · 1 answered*.
- **🕒 The release notes could be sent *now* or not at all, so a deploy now has a clock: they go out at 03:33 IST, with 04:00 and 04:44 behind them because the free host that keeps this bot alive can be asleep at the first**: *"remove from sending update in meow update channel … send the msg at 3:33 ist if u miss this time then 4 am or 4:44 am ist"*. The notes are the bot talking about itself in a channel it is in, and they fired at whatever hour somebody happened to push — a note at 14:20 is a note in somebody's working day, on every deploy, to every server that ever bound an update channel. There was no request a server could make, either: clearing the binding reads as *not chosen yet* rather than *not wanted*, so `bot_channels.ensure_bound` bound the channel by name again on the next boot, and the home server receives notes through `LEGACY_UPDATE_CHANNEL_ID` even with nothing bound, which is why a checkout running locally can post one into that channel. **So the switch became a switch plus a clock.** `UPDATE_WINDOW_IST` (default `03:33,04:00,04:44`) is the times of day, in IST, a note may go out; `always` is the old behaviour for anyone who wants it; and `SILENT_UPDATES=1` is the other end — not *later* but *never*, which is the only setting that leaves a server's update channel permanently empty. **A held note is owed, not dropped, and that is the property that makes the delay safe.** Nothing is recorded as announced unless Discord accepted the message, so a boot outside the window sends nothing *and* records nothing; the note that goes out at 03:33 is `summarize_changelog(_changelog_since_last(...))`, i.e. everything since that server's last one, so nine deploys in a day are one complete note rather than eight lost ones — a delay is a delay and never a gap. **The clock is `utils/version.py`'s, and the reason it is a fixed +05:30 is the host.** A slim container has no tzdata, so a `zoneinfo` lookup that raises would quietly compute the window in the host's own time — UTC on the deploy, half a day from where it was aimed — and India has had one offset since 1945, so the constant is not an approximation of IST, it is IST. A window stays open for thirty minutes once it arrives, which is what makes a process the monitor woke at 03:34 count as in time, and a window that opens at 23:50 is still open after midnight (the arithmetic is minutes-of-day for exactly that reason). **One rule, two callers.** The boot still calls `_announce_release_notes`, which is now also reached through the client as `bot.announce_release_notes` by `keep_alive.release_window_loop` — the same silence check, the same per-server records, the same legacy channel — because a loop with its own copy of the announcement would be a second way to post a note that the first one cannot see. A retry after a window's failed post waits five minutes rather than thirty seconds, so sixty attempts in one window cannot become the thing the channel remembers about a deploy, and two existing tests now pin the clock wide open: a suite that is about the time of day must not pass or fail depending on what time the suite runs. **22 new tests** (`tests/test_update_window.py`), including the one that reads `render.yaml` to check the window the deploy declares is the window the code runs, and the one that asserts holding a note writes no record. `render.yaml`, `.env.example` and `docs/deployment.md` all carry the pair, with what each end costs.
- **⏳ "Free and online forever" is three things, and only one of them is in this repository — so the one that is not is now written down where it is needed**: *"MAKE IT Free and online forever so even if I leave the project the bot should stay running and not die"*. **Something outside the host has to knock on it.** A free Render instance is stopped after fifteen minutes without an inbound request, and nothing inside the container can prevent that — the timer is on their side, so a self-ping is a bot asking itself to stay awake while the thing measuring it sits somewhere else. The outside knock this deployment already reads is UptimeRobot's free plan (fifty monitors, five-minute intervals, and the same read-only key that turns the dashboard's status panel into history), pointed at `/health`, which is public on purpose and answers counts rather than names for exactly this reason. **The hours are a budget.** 750 instance hours a month against the 730–744 an always-on service spends, so this bot may be the only free service on the account: a second one running the same way suspends both, and the symptom is "the bot died", which is the worst kind of report because it is wrong. The public site and the owner console are on Cloudflare Pages, which has no such budget. **And the disk is temporary, which is survivable rather than fatal**: Render wipes the filesystem on every deploy, and the database on it is restored from Supabase at boot, snapshotted hourly, and — the part that matters for a host that sleeps — flushed to Supabase on `SIGTERM`, the signal Render sends *before* it stops an idle instance, so a nap is a save rather than a loss. The shrunken-database guard is what stops a wiped local file from being uploaded over the good copy after a bad boot. All three are now `docs/deployment.md`'s *Free, and online without you*, with the parts this does **not** cover said out loud (a hard kill with no warning, and a deployment where the Supabase pair was never set). The README's troubleshooting row for "free host sleeps/stops" now names the monitor instead of pointing at a document that never mentioned one.
- **🖱️ The Cursor panel never opened on the owner site — the rail was told to clip, the panel was told to open *outside* the rail, and both instructions landed on the same element**: *"The Fix aren't there the cursor panel is fucked in owner site"*. `.rail` clips (`overflow: hidden`, added so its seven nav groups and pinned footer could not paint over the page beside them), while `.customizer-popover` was `position: absolute; left: calc(100% + 12px)` — deliberately *outside* the rail, which is the one place a clipping ancestor does not allow. Clicking **Cursor** drew nothing at all, so the control read as broken rather than as a gesture with a hidden result. **The panel now leaves the clipping context instead of arguing with it**: it renders through a React portal on `document.body` with `position: fixed`, placed from the button's own `getBoundingClientRect()` — measured rather than hardcoded, which also covers the case the old `@media (max-width: 860px)` rule existed for, because on a narrow window there is no room beside the rail and it opens above the button instead — and re-placed on resize and scroll so it cannot be left behind. Console rebuilt, `tsc --noEmit` clean, source stamp `cd3108015e0b`.
- **❓ `/qna` — an anonymous question box, where the promise is one sentence and the code is the sentence**: *"Build the /qna feature: an anonymous question relay driven by an environment-variable webhook, a way for you to reply that DMs the asker, and the asker's name visible only to you so the anonymity promise stays true."* A member runs `/qna ask`, types their question into a form, and the question appears in the server's channel posted by a **webhook** (`QNA_WEBHOOK_URL`) whose name is *Anonymous* — not a disguised member, an absent one, with `allowed_mentions` empty so a question cannot ping `@everyone`. The owner gets a **DM about the same question that does name the asker**, with an Answer button on it; the answer is **DMed to the asker** and never posted, because an answer in the channel would tell everybody that somebody asked, which is the one fact the asker was promised away. **The asymmetry is enforced, not described.** The public body is built by `utils/qna.relay_payload` from a closed field list — the question's id, its text, the time — and the test builds a payload from a row whose asker is unmistakable and fails if the name or the id appears anywhere in what the webhook is handed; the name is rendered in exactly two places, the owner's DM and `/qna inbox`, and both refuse anyone who is not `OWNER_USER_ID` (checked in the callback, because Discord has no permission bit that means "this one account"). The public handle is **derived** — `anon 4F2A` is the question's id in hex — so there is no second identifier to correlate and nothing that can collide. **Three failure modes are answered rather than assumed.** The question is written down *before* the relay is attempted, so a webhook that refuses the post leaves a row saying so instead of losing it, and the asker is told the box refused it; the answer is **claimed before the DM is sent** (one conditional `UPDATE`, so two clicks on an old card cannot DM somebody twice) and **put back in the inbox** if the DM itself fails, because an answer that never arrived has not been given; and an unset `QNA_WEBHOOK_URL` makes `/qna ask` say which setting is missing rather than doing nothing, which is the state that reads as a broken bot. **Two Discord mechanics decide the shapes here, both read out of the installed library rather than guessed at**: a modal's submit is dispatched *only* to a modal the client already knows about, and `send_modal` registers it by `custom_id` — so the answer form cannot use a fixed `custom_id` (two forms open at once would overwrite each other and one would answer the wrong question) and the question id rides in that field instead; and a button on an old message is dispatched by `custom_id` once the process that sent it is gone, so the Answer button carries **no state at all** and the card's *message id* is what finds the question — one view registered at boot answers every card ever sent, however old. The DM form is registered for dispatch by the modal itself, which is why this feature adds no `on_interaction` listener and no per-question view. **43 new tests** (`tests/test_qna.py`, 2471 total, 0 skipped), the registry now reads **15 systems and 96 commands** (`docs/discord-directory.md` follows it, as that file's own guard requires), and `PRIVACY.md` §1 plus `docs/legal/data-requests.md` name the row — the question, the answer, and who asked, kept until deleted, rendered only to the owner.
- **🖼️ The operator console's own logo was a broken image on five of its nine themes — and the reason it could happen is that a component was deciding for itself which files exist**: *"check the site i think its fucked all 5 site the logo aren't loading"*. `Shell.tsx` drew the brand mark as `/static/logo-<theme>.svg`, which is a *promise* that somebody drew that file; the theme vocabulary grew from four to nine in `588d41f` and only four marks were ever made, so **Aurora Borealis, Cosmic Nebula, Cyber Plasma, Sunset Flare and Synthwave** each asked for a file that does not exist and were handed the browser's broken-image glyph, next to the bot's own name, in the rail. **There is nothing wrong with the missing marks** — four themes having their own logo and five using the default is a perfectly good design. What was wrong was the console assuming, theme by theme, that a file it never checked was there. The path now comes from `logoFor(theme)` in the module that owns the theme vocabulary, so **one door** decides, and the rule is *checked rather than trusted*: `tests/test_console_brand.py` walks `THEMES` against `static/` **in both directions** and fails on a theme that has a mark but is not declared, or is declared without a file — proven by temporarily declaring `aurora` and watching the guard name it as the offender. Two halves came with it. The `<img>` gained a guarded `onError` fallback, because the *static* owner build is a second host that could have failed to copy a mark and a missing file should degrade to the real logo rather than to a glyph. And `scripts/build_owner.py` now **discovers** `logo-*.svg` instead of listing four by hand — a listed file is a promise somebody has to remember, and that is the exact shape of this bug. The outbound law that forbids a component building an image URL by hand was widened to admit `logoFor()` *and* strengthened: it is the second door into an `src`, so it is asserted the way `iconSrc` is — one interpolation, and it is the theme id. `tsc --noEmit` clean, bundle re-stamped `05d318ba0320`. 2428 tests + 1312 subtests pass, 7 new.
- **📻 The radio was six stations, so the fallback was a formality — it is now 122 across 54 genres and ten languages, reachable by a search rather than a menu, and every one of them was fetched before it was written down**: *"in 2nd one upgrade it add more things more features more station and many more like more types instrumental music type language more etc…"*. Radio is the answer to *"the platforms will not serve this host"*, and a six-item answer is not an answer: somebody who wanted Turkish pop, baroque, chiptune or **a language they actually speak** was still told no. **The picker had to stop being a picker first.** Discord caps a command's fixed choices at 25 and renders them in declaration order, so a menu is a catalogue nobody can find *Turkish* in — which is why the station option is now an **autocomplete over the words people have** (name, genre, language, mood), with `/music stations` as the browse command for a reader who does not know a word to type. Moods are searched in the words people *use*: nobody searches for `focus`, they search for *study*, so a deliberately small synonym table maps one to the other and is discounted a rank, so it can never displace a literal match. **Two rules govern what may be added, both from failures this project has already had.** Every URL was **fetched before it was written down** — 122 live out of roughly 200 candidates, with a 404, a DNS failure and an HTML error page all rejected — and every station is named **what the stream calls itself**, not what a directory called it: the pass that built this found the entry filed under *baroque* was a Bee Gees station and the one under *classical* was Qur'an recitation, both fine stations and neither what the label promised. **And the weekly self-check could not see 91 of the 122.** `utils/media_health` decides whether a literal is media partly by its **extension**, and a radio stream frequently has none — `https://stream.laut.fm/lofi` is a host and a word — so the module written to stop dead links rotting silently would have declined to check most of the new catalogue, which is its own bug one level down. The fix is a **declared** `STREAM_HOSTS` list (ports included, because they are part of the comparison), which leaves the scanner's contract untouched and puts every station into the probe by *having been written down*. **`/music radio` with no argument now replays what the server chose last** — a new `music_radio` row, written best-effort because remembering is not worth a failure — and the first-ever invocation picks one and *says that it picked* instead of pretending the choice was the member's. **Separately, the failure that named the wrong cause is fixed.** A refusal was being raised over requests that had *no recording to play*: *"YouTube is refusing this host's address right now"* about a search SoundCloud answered with four edits and Audius with three remixes — a true sentence pointing at the wrong problem, since nothing was wrong with this host's reach. The message now names both facts, and adds the second clause only when it is true of **every** provider asked (*"and the other sources have nothing but edits, covers or remixes of it"* / *"…do not have it"*), because a claim this code cannot support is worse than a shorter sentence. Anything that fails as `blocked` also now names the door that still opens, since the radio touches no provider at all. 2422 tests + 1303 subtests pass, 36 new in `tests/test_radio.py`.
- **🎧 The bot says *brb* when music takes it out of somebody's room — and the owner console's rail can no longer draw itself on top of the page**: *"fix the ui ux of the msg and other things and send a msg like brb when person plays music in vc b"*. **The message is about a silence, not a missing feature.** The two voice features share one connection because one Discord client sits in one voice channel — so when somebody in *another* room presses play, music moves the bot out of the room it was sitting in, and the person left behind watches it leave and is told nothing. Unsaid, that reads as *"it left because of me"* when the truth is a two-room errand it was asked to run. So it says so, in its own voice: *brb — somebody asked for music in another room, so I'm on speaker duty*, with a footer saying when it is back and who can turn the behaviour off. **Three deliberate refusals in one small card.** Nothing is sent when the music started in the room the bot was *already in* — that person is now less alone than they were, and a goodbye for a song playing in their own channel is noise, so the condition is the channel *changing* rather than music starting at all. Nothing is sent to a server that chose silent arrivals, because it asked for silence and the greeting's own setting is the place that decision already lives. And it names **no command**, held to the same rule the greeting's footer is: it lands in front of whoever was alone, and the switch is a Manage Server command Discord hides from their picker entirely, so every variant and the footer are now asserted to contain no slash token. **The console bug was a column that could not hold itself.** `.rail` is a fixed `100vh` box with no clipping, and its content is taller than that on a laptop — seven nav groups plus a footer pinned to the bottom — so the footer *escaped the box* and painted over the grid's other column, which is exactly how the appearance swatches and the Cursor button ended up sitting on top of the Overview's own action buttons. The fix is clipping rather than a taller box, since the rail has to fit the viewport: the column no longer lets anything out, the brand and the footer are fixed-size, and the page list — the one flexible child — scrolls inside it instead of pushing the footer out. Console rebuilt, `tsc --noEmit` clean, source stamp `3a10af3a86f4`. 2386 tests + 930 subtests pass, 4 new.
- **🚪 The card asked *which of these did you mean* and then failed to play the one that was picked, because the menu came from a **search** — and a search is the half of the exchange a hostile provider still answers**: *"Why its showing option when I gave him the link and it takes soo long after selcting and error"*, over a Spotify link that resolved, a shortlist of YouTube uploads, and a `DownloadError` after choosing one. **The bot had already said the answer out loud** — *YouTube is refusing this host's address right now* — and then offered a YouTube-only menu, which is the same fact ignored twice in a row. The reason the two are compatible is that they are two different requests: a flat search is cheap and unauthenticated and keeps working, while only the **stream** extraction is refused. So a provider can answer a search and have nothing it can hand over, and the chain returned its shortlist the moment it saw candidates — it never checked that the provider could play any of them. **Now the menu is only offered once its provider has proved it can hand over its own top choice.** That is not extra work: the alternatives carry no stream URL, so choosing one cost an extraction *anyway*, after the member had waited and after the card had promised — the same extraction, moved earlier, where it discovers a refusal while there is still a provider left to try and leaves the likeliest choice already resolved and fresh by the time it is picked. A provider already inside its refusal cooldown is skipped outright rather than probed, since that answer was established minutes ago and re-establishing it costs seconds on every song. **Building this surfaced a worse one behind it.** SoundCloud's answer to a mainstream single was a **30-second preview** of the real upload plus four edits — a *Slowed*, a *Piano & String Version* remix, an *Acoustic Version* and a *Mashup* — and after the unusable preview was excluded, three of those wrong recordings were offered as a choice. A set made **entirely** of other recordings is not a shortlist to pick from; it is a provider with nothing, and the next one is asked. So with YouTube refused, the member now gets one sentence in about **4.6 seconds** — naming the platform that is refusing the host, which is the fact they can act on — instead of a four-item menu of remixes that ends in an error. Worth writing down plainly, because it is the question the report really asks: **a Spotify address cannot play anything by itself.** Spotify hands over metadata and no audio, so a link there is a metadata fetch followed by a *search*, which is why a link can ask at all. 2382 tests + 930 subtests pass, 4 new.
- **🌐 A refused address now has a third answer that needs no account at all — an outbound proxy, applied to **both halves** of the request, because a provider signs its stream URL for whoever asked for it**: *"Add an outbound proxy setting so yt-dlp can reach YouTube from this datacenter host without cookies"*, following the pass that measured the refusal itself. Cookies need a throwaway login and a PO token needs a token provider; a proxy asks nothing of an account, which is what makes it the answer for an operator who has neither. **The half-fix is the whole problem.** A ``googlevideo`` URL is signed for the address that asked for it, so a proxy that runs the extraction and not the fetch hands back a URL *this host cannot open* — the stream resolves, the card names the track, and then nothing is heard, which reads as a provider being unreliable rather than as a setting applied to one half of a pair. So the same setting is handed to ffmpeg as ``-http_proxy`` on the input side, where an input option belongs, and the pairing is asserted rather than assumed. **It is deliberately not applied to everything.** Radio and the credential-free catalogue are the two sources that do not refuse this host, and radio is the fallback that exists to keep playing when the extractor cannot — so it is the one thing that must not acquire a new way to fail. The rule is derived from the provider that actually served the stream instead of being listed, which also keeps ``-http_proxy`` off inputs that are not HTTP at all, where an unknown protocol option is an error in its own right. **A SOCKS URL is declined with its reason rather than half-honoured**, and that is a measured decision: ffmpeg has no SOCKS support in any version, so a SOCKS proxy would carry the extraction and leave the fetch behind — precisely the failure above, arriving from a setting that looked configured. The reason is printed once per process and carried on the operators' sources reading next to a boolean; the URL itself never is, because it holds a credential, so it is a declared secret and the module gained a redaction helper for anything it prints. **The law guarding this surface caught the new work, and then caught something older**: a setting read through a constant is invisible to the config audit's search, and ``YTDL_SOURCE_ORDER`` had been readable, undeclared **and** unfindable all at once — an operator reading the report was never told that setting existed. Both names are now spelled out where they are read. **Proven with a real proxy rather than a mocked one**: ffmpeg fetched through a local one from an origin address that is not reachable at all, exited 0, and the proxy logged the absolute-form request line, which is the evidence that it was used *as* a proxy. 2378 tests + 930 subtests pass, 13 new.
- **🔊 The echo had a number, and the number was the channel's own bitrate: every song that was not already Opus was being decoded and re-encoded down to **64 kbps** on any server without a boost, discarding more than half of a recording that had arrived intact — measured at **8.9 dB** of fidelity thrown away, and now recovered**: *"the quality is like echo and shitty ass — find a method that provides highest quality and best music and no error"*, over a now-playing card reading `ffmpeg re-encode [Normal (studio clean)]`. Three separate things were wrong on the one path this deployment actually plays through, and the first of them is a one-line condition that had never been exercised. **The pass-through path was unreachable.** It is the whole high-quality route — the provider's own Opus packets handed on untouched, no encoder in the loop at all — and it required the source to be *no louder than the channel*.
